It is then crucial to understand how the second Fueter operator acts on negative
*
-powers, and this is done in the next result. To ease the notation we shall write
*
instead of
*_{p,R}=*_{q,L}
.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let
p,q\in\mathbb{H}
be such that
q\notin[p]
, and let
\Delta
denote the Laplace operator with respect to the variable
q
. Then for
n\geq 1
we have

(8.1)

\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}=-4\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q).

Proof.

By (
2.52
) we have

\Delta(q-p)^{-*}=-\Delta S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)=4(p-\bar{q})\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-2}(q),

while, on the right hand side of the equality (
8.1
), we have

-4\left(\sum_{j=0}^{1}(1-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-2}(q)=4(p-\bar{q})\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-2}(q),

and this proves the assertion for
n=1
.
We suppose that formula (
8.1
) is true for
n
, and we prove it for
n+1
. By (
2.29
), (
5.6
) and the inductive hypothesis we have

\displaystyle\Delta(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\Delta S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\partial_{q_{0}}\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}\left[\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)^{2}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j-1)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}(n-j)j(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j-1)}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(n+1)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)(2q_{0}-2p)\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q).

Since
\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)=(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)
and
2q_{0}-2p=(q-p)+(\bar{q}-p)
, and since we are considering the left
*
-product with respect to
q
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}\left[\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)^{2}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-1-j)(j+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(n+1)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(n+1)\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right)\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}\left(n^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}-n\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}j(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(j+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{j=0}^{n}n(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}-\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}-\sum_{j=0}^{n}n(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}\left(-\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(j+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j+1)}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}j(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-n\sum_{j=0}^{n}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}-\sum_{j=0}^{n}n(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\left(\sum_{j=0}^{n+1}(n+1-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q).

This proves the result.
∎

Using Theorem
8.2
we can write

(8.2)

\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}=-4\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q),

where

(8.3)

\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*j},

and with this notation we get an interesting expression for the powers of the conjugate Fueter operator
\bar{D}^{n}
applied to the
F
-kernel, see (
2.52
).

Corollary 8.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
q
,
p\in\mathbb{H}
such that
q\notin[p]
. Then

(8.4)

\bar{D}^{n}F_{L}(p,q)=2^{n+2}n!(-1)^{n}\mathcal{M}_{n+1}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-2}(q).

Proof.

By (
2.28
), Remark
2.19
and (
2.52
) we have

\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}=\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q).

Since the kernel
F_{L}(p,q)
is axially Fueter regular in
q
we can replace
\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}
with
\frac{\bar{D}^{n-1}}{2^{n-1}}
, and by (
8.2
) we get the result.
∎

Remark 8.4
.

If we consider
p=0
in (
8.4
) we get a nicer formula. Indeed, since
F_{L}(0,q)=4E(q)
and
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,0)=S_{n}(q)
we have

\bar{D}^{n}E(q)=2^{n}n!(-1)^{n}S_{n+1}(q)|q|^{-2(n+2)},\qquad q\neq 0,

where

(8.5)

S_{n}(q)=\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,0)=\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)\bar{q}^{n-j}q^{j},\qquad n\geq 0.

In the next result we list the main properties of the functions
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
.

Proposition 8.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
,
q\in\mathbb{H}
such that
q\notin[p]
. Then:

1)

\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)
is an axially Fueter regular function in
q
.

2)

\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
is a left slice polyanalytic function of order
n+1
in
q
and is right slice hyperholomorphic in
p
.

3)

If
q\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
rewrites as

(8.6)

\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)=\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right]

Proof.

We prove the various statements listed above.

1)

By (
8.2
), the fact that the function
(q-p)^{-*n}
is slice hyperholomorphic for
q\notin[p]
and the Fueter theorem we get that the function
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}
is axially Fueter regular in
q
.

2)

Arguments similar to those used in Proposition
5.4
prove that the
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
are left slice polyanalytic functions of order
n+1
in
q
. The fact that the
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
are right slice hyperholomorphic in
p
follows from Proposition
2.10
.

3)

We prove the result by induction on
n
. If
n=1
we have

\mathcal{M}_{1}(q,p)=\bar{q}-p.

On the right-hand side of formula (
8.6
) we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2(\bar{q}-p)^{*2}+(\underline{q})^{-1}(q-\bar{q})(p^{2}-2q_{0}p+|q|^{2})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{2}\left(2\bar{q}p-\bar{q}^{2}-2q_{0}p+|q|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{2}\left(-2\underline{q}p+2|\underline{q}|^{2}+2q_{0}\underline{q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{q}-p,

and this proves the formula for
n=1
. We suppose that formula (
8.6
) is valid for
n
and we prove it for
n+1
. By the definition of the left
*
-product in
q
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n+1}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n+1}(n+1-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{n}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}+\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)*\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-j)}*(q-p)^{*(j-1)}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{n}(n-j)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1-j)}*(q-p)^{*j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\mathcal{H}_{n}(q,p)+\bar{q}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)-\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)p,

where the functions
\mathcal{H}_{n}(q,p)
are defined in (
6.2
).
By (
6.3
) and the inductive hypothesis we have

(8.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n+1}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{2}\left[(q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)

\displaystyle+\frac{\bar{q}(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right]

\displaystyle-\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right]p

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[4\underline{q}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+2\underline{q}(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2n\bar{q}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\bar{q}(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}p-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)p\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[2(q-\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+(q-\bar{q})(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+2)}+\bar{q}(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)p\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2(n+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+2)}+q(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)p-2(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}p+2q(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2(n+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+2)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}(q-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)+\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\underline{q})^{-1}q(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)+(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)p-2(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}p+2q(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2(n+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+2)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}(q-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}*(q-p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)+2(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)\right].

Recalling that
\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)=(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)
we deduce

(8.8)

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}*(q-p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)+2(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)^{*2}+2(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)*\left(q-p-2\underline{q}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}*(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q).

Finally, by plugging (
8.8
) into (
8.7
) we get

\mathcal{M}_{n+1}(q,p)=\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2(n+1)(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+2)}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left((q-p)^{*(n+1)}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)\right],

which ends the proof.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

If we set
p=0
in (
8.2
), we obtain

(8.9)

\Delta(q^{-n})=-4|q|^{-2(n+1)}S_{n}(q),

where the polynomials
S_{n}(q)
are defined in (
8.5
).
If
q\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathbb{R}
, the polynomials
S_{n}(q)
admit the following closed-form expression:

(8.10)

S_{n}(q)=\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{4}\left[-2n\bar{q}^{n+1}+(\underline{q})^{-1}|q|^{2}\left(q^{n}-\bar{q}^{n}\right)\right].

Now we show that the Laurent Fueter regular series of
f
and
\breve{f}=\Delta f
have the same set of convergence and an interesting compact expression.

Theorem 8.7
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be an axially symmetric open set and let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in
\Omega
that admits
*
-Laurent expansion at
p\in\mathbb{H}

(8.11)

f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n},\qquad\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H},

convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in

\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})\subset\Omega
,
where
\frac{1}{R_{2}}=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}
and
R_{1}=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{-n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}
. Then we can write the Laurent Fueter regular series of
\breve{f}=\Delta f
, which is convergent in
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
, as

(8.12)

\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)c_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)c_{-n},

where
\{c_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{-2(n+1)(n+2)a_{n+2}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{c_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{-4a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Proof.

We apply the Laplace operator to (
8.11
). By (
2.29
), (
7.9
) and (
8.2
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Delta(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+2)(n+1)\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)a_{n+2}-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}.

The result follows by setting the coefficients
\{c_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
as in the statement. We now study the convergence of both the series in (
8.12
). The convergence of the first series follows by Theorem
7.10
, so we focus on the second series. Since the case
q\in\mathbb{R}
is immediate, we consider the case
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. By formulas (
8.2
), (
4.16
) and Theorem
2.6
we have

\displaystyle-4\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\underline{q}^{-1}I_{\underline{q}}\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{-*(n+1)}-(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}\right]a_{-n}

\displaystyle-n(\underline{q})^{-1}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{-(n+1)}-(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}\right]a_{-n}

\displaystyle-I_{\underline{q}}(\underline{q})^{-2}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{-n}-(q_{I}-p)^{-n}\right]a_{-n},

where
q_{I}=x+yI
and
q_{-I}=x-yI
are chosen on
\mathbb{C}_{I}
, with
p\in\mathbb{C}_{I}
. This implies

\displaystyle|4\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n|\underline{q}|^{-1}\left[\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{-(n+1)}a_{-n}\right|+\left|(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}a_{-n}\right|\right]

\displaystyle+|\underline{q}|^{-2}\left[\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{-n}a_{-n}\right|+\left|(q_{I}-p)^{-n}a_{-n}\right|\right].

By the hypothesis on the coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n<0}
and the fact that
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
we get that the series

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{-*(n+1)}a_{-n}|,\qquad\hbox{and}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}|

are both convergent and this ends the proof.

∎

As for the harmonic regular series also the Laurent Fueter regular series can also be expressed in terms of the
F
-kernel, see (
2.52
), and of the slice hyperholomorphic Cauchy kernel.

Proposition 8.8
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in an axially symmetric open set
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{H}
that admits a
*
-Laurent expansion at
p\in\mathbb{H}
as in (
2.27
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
and assume that it is convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})\subseteq\Omega
, with
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
as in Theorem
8.7
. Then, for
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
the Laurent Fueter regular series of
\breve{f}=\Delta f
can be written as

(8.13)

\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\bar{D}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)b_{-n},

with
\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{-2(n+1)(n+2)a_{n+2}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{b_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{(-1)^{n}2^{1-n}a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Proof.

Bearing in mind that we can apply the Laplacian term by term, by formula (
2.29
) we can write the expansion in series of
\breve{f}=\Delta f
as

\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Delta(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\Delta S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)a_{-n}.

By (
7.9
) and (
2.52
) we get

\Delta f(q)=-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+2)(n+1)\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)a_{n+2}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)a_{-n}.

Since the
F
-kernel
F_{L}(p,q)
is axially Fueter regular in the variable
q
we get that
\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)=\frac{1}{2^{n-1}}\bar{D}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)
. Finally by setting the coefficients
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
as in the statement we obtain (
8.13
).
∎

Remark 8.9
.

If we set
p=0
in (
8.13
), and use the identity
F_{L}(0,q)=4E(q)
, we obtain the following expression for the Laurent Fueter regular series in a neighbourhood of the origin:

(8.14)

\Delta f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4}{(n-1)!}\bar{D}^{n-1}E(q)b_{-n},

We also note that we can write the Laurent Fueter regular series in terms of the pseudo-Cauchy kernel and the slice hyperholomorphic Cauchy kernel by using (
2.56
) and (
2.62
), respectively.

In
[
29
]
the authors prove that the
F
-kernel has the following series expansion in terms of Clifford-Appell polynomials:

(8.15)

F_{L}(p,q)=-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)(n+2)\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)p^{-3-n},\qquad|q|<|p|.

This paves the way to get another equivalent expression of the Laurent Fueter regular series.

Proposition 8.10
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in an axially symmetric open set
\Omega
in
\mathbb{H}
that admits a
*
-Laurent expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p\in\mathbb{H}
as in (
2.27
), with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})\subseteq\Omega
. Assume moreover that the region
|q|<|p|
has nonempty intersection with
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then, on that intersection, the Laurent Fueter regular series of
\breve{f}
, can be written as

(8.16)

\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}(k+1)_{2}\binom{k}{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-3-k}b_{-n},

where
\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{-2(n+1)(n+2)a_{n+2}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{b_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{(-1)^{n}2^{1-n}a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Moreover, for
q\notin\mathbb{R}
we have

\Delta f(q)=-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right)a_{n}.

Proof.

By using repeatedly the Appell property of the polynomials
\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)
, see (
2.46
), we get

(8.17)

\bar{D}^{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)=2^{n-1}\frac{k!}{(k-n+1)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q),\qquad k\geq n-1.

Now, we assume that the intersection of the region
|q|<|p|
with the domain of convergence of the
*
-Taylor expansion is nonempty. From Proposition
8.8
, the expansion in series of the
F
-kernel, see (
8.15
), and (
8.17
) we get

\displaystyle\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\bar{D}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)b_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(k+1)(k+2)}{(n-1)!}\left(\bar{D}^{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)\right)p^{-3-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}2^{n-1}\frac{(k+1)(k+2)k!}{(k-n+1)!(n-1)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-3-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)b_{n}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}(k+1)_{2}\binom{k}{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-3-k}2^{n-1}b_{-n}.

This proves (
8.16
).
We consider
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. First we find a closed expression of the second series in (
8.12
). By Proposition
6.9
and by (
8.6
) we have

(8.18)

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}a_{-n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left((q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}\right)

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left((\bar{q}-p)^{-*n}-(q-p)^{-*n}\right)a_{-n}.

Hence by Theorem
8.7
and Proposition
7.12
we have

\displaystyle\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)(n+2)\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)a_{n+2}-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n}

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left((\bar{q}-p)^{-*n}-(q-p)^{-*n}\right)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left((\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right)a_{n},

as stated.

∎

8.2.
Laurent Fueter regular spherical series

To discuss the Laurent series written in terms of the polynomials of spherical type, we introduce the following notion.

Definition 8.11
.

Let
\Omega
be an axially symmetric open set in
\mathbb{H}
and let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in
\Omega
that admits a spherical Laurent expansion centered at
p
and convergent in a suitable set contained in
\Omega
. Then we say that
\breve{f}=\Delta f
has a Laurent Fueter regular spherical series at
p
.

The above definition is justified by the fact that applying the second Fueter map to the
expression of the spherical series of a function
f
we get a formula for the regular Fueter
spherical series.

Theorem 8.12
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in a neighborhood of
p\in\mathbb{H}
containing
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
, for some
R_{2}>R_{1}>0
, where it admits a spherical Laurent expansion (
2.39
), convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact
subsets) in a Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Let
\breve{f}
be the axially Fueter regular function in a neighbourhood of
p
given by
\breve{f}=\Delta f
. Then,
\breve{f}
has the following Laurent Fueter regular spherical series expansion

(8.19)

\displaystyle\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!-16(q_{0}-p_{0})^{2}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{C}_{n}(q,p)\left[a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right]

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\!-8(q_{0}-p_{0})\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)\,a_{2n+1}-4\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}n\,Q^{n-1}_{p}(q)\left[a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right],

where
\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)
and
\mathcal{C}_{n}(q,p)
are defined in (
5.14
) and (
7.20
), respectively.

Proof.

Note that a Cassini shell with
R_{1}>0
does not meet
[p]
, so that all the terms are well defined. We apply the operator
\Delta
term by term to the Laurent spherical expansion (
2.39
). The interchange of
\Delta
and the summation will be justified later in the proof. We first consider the case
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. By (
4.24
), formulas (
7.24
) and (
7.25
) remain valid for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Hence, substituting these identities into (
2.39
), we obtain (
8.19
).
We now justify the interchange of
\Delta
and the summation. Let
K

be a compact subset of
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. There exist
\rho_{1},\rho_{2}
such that
R_{1}<\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}<R_{2}
, so
\rho_{1}^{2}\leq|Q_{p}(q)|\leq\rho_{2}^{2}
for
q\in K

For
n\geq 1
, we have

|\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)|\leq n\rho_{2}^{2n-2}
, and
|\mathcal{C}_{n}(q,p)|\leq\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\rho_{2}^{2n-4}
. Whereas, for
n\leq-1
, we have
|\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)|\leq|n|\rho_{1}^{2n-2}
and
|\mathcal{C}_{n}(q,p)|\leq\frac{|n|(|n|+1)}{2}\rho_{1}^{2n-4}
.
Therefore, all the series appearing in the previous calculations converge absolutely and uniformly on compact subsets of
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
.

It remains to consider the real points. Let

q=q_{0}\in U(p,R_{1},R_{2})\cap\mathbb{R}
. Then

\mathcal{L}_{n}(q_{0},p)=nQ_{p}^{n-1}(q_{0}),\qquad\mathcal{C}_{n}(q_{0},p)=\frac{n(n-1)}{2}Q_{p}^{n-2}(q_{0}),\qquad n\in\mathbb{Z}.

Moreover, formulas (
7.26
) and (
7.27
) remain valid for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. Therefore, at the real points, the right-hand side of (
8.19
) coincides with the series obtained by applying
\Delta
term by term to (
2.39
).
∎

Proposition 8.13
.

Under the hypotheses of Theorem
8.12
, let
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be such that

R_{1}:=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{-n}|^{\frac{1}{n}},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\frac{1}{R_{2}}:=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}},\qquad R_{1}<R_{2}.

The Laurent Fueter regular spherical series centered at
p
converges (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in the Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
,
where
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
, with
I\in\mathbb{S}
.

Proof.

Let
K
be a compact subset of the Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then there exist
r_{1},r_{2}
such that
R_{1}<r_{1}<r_{2}<R_{2}
and
r_{1}^{2n}<|Q_{p}^{n}(q)|<r_{2}^{2n}
for
q\in K
. Choose
R^{\prime}
and
R^{\prime\prime}
such that
R_{1}<R^{\prime}<r_{1}<r_{2}<R^{\prime\prime}<R_{2}
. By the assumptions on the coefficients, we have
|a_{n}|\leq(1/R^{\prime\prime})^{n}
and
|a_{-n}|\leq(R^{\prime})^{n}
for all sufficiently large
n
. Using Theorem
8.12
we can write the Laurent Fueter regular spherical series as

\displaystyle\Delta f(q)={-16\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{n+2}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})^{2}\left[a_{2n+4}+(q-p)a_{2n+5}\right]}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)Q_{p}^{n}(q)\left[a_{2n+2}+(q-p)a_{2n+3}\right]{-8\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})a_{2n+3}}

\displaystyle{-16\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{C}_{-n}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})^{2}\left[a_{-2n}+(q-p)a_{-2n+1}\right]}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}nQ_{p}^{-n-1}(q)[a_{-2n}+(q-p)a_{-2n+1}]{-8\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})a_{-2n+1}}.

The convergence of the first three series follows by Theorem
7.19
, so we focus on the remaining ones.
By (
7.20
), (
5.14
) on
K
we have,

|\mathcal{C}_{-n}(q,p)|\leq\frac{n(n+1)}{2}\,r_{1}^{-2n-4},\qquad|\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)|\leq n\,r_{1}^{-2n-2},

so that in the estimates below one may replace
2\mathcal{C}_{-n}(q,p)
by
n(n+1)Q^{-n-2}_{p}(q)
and
\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)
by
nQ^{-n-1}_{p}(q)
, as far as moduli are concerned.
By Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
we get

\displaystyle n(n+1)\left|Q_{p}^{-n-2}(q)(q_{0}-p_{0})^{2}\left[a_{-2n}+(q-p)a_{-2n+1}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n(n+1)}{R^{\prime}r_{1}^{4}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}}{r_{1}}\right)^{2n}(r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2})\left(R^{\prime}+\left(\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}\right)\right)=:A_{n},

and

\displaystyle n|Q_{p}^{-n-1}(q)[a_{-2n}+(q-p)a_{-2n+1}]|\leq\frac{n}{R^{\prime}r_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}}{r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\left(R^{\prime}+\left(\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}\right)\right):=B_{n},

and

\displaystyle|nQ_{p}^{-n-1}(q)(q_{0}-p_{0})a_{-2n+1}|\leq\frac{n}{R^{\prime}r_{1}^{2}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}}{r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}:=C_{n}.

By the ratio test we get that the series
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(A_{n}+B_{n}+C_{n})
is convergent and this proves the result.
∎

In suitable sets, it is possible to write the Laurent Fueter regular spherical series in terms of the functions
S_{n}(q)
defined in (
8.5
) or the Clifford-Appell polynomials
\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)
, see (
2.45
), as proved below.

Proposition 8.14
.

Let
q
,
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
. We assume that
f
is a slice hyperholomorphic function that admits a spherical Laurent expansion at
p
as in (
2.39
), with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
,
0<R_{1}<R_{2}
. Assume moreover that the region
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
has nonempty intersection with
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then, on that intersection, the Laurent Fueter regular spherical series of
\breve{f}
can be written as

(8.20)

\displaystyle\breve{f}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta f(q)=A_{T}(q,p)-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})|(q-p_{0})|^{-4(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}Ia_{-2n+1},

If instead the region
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
has nonempty intersection with
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
then, on that intersection, another way of writing the Laurent–Fueter regular spherical series of
\breve{f}
is given by:

(8.21)

\displaystyle\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{T}(q,p)+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+3)\mathcal{Q}_{2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)-1}Ia_{-2n+1}

where
A_{T}(q,p)
is its Taylor part given by (
7.30
).

Proof.

By hypothesis we can write
f
as in (
6.24
). The Taylor part of (
8.20
) can be written as in Theorem
7.20
. Thus, we focus on showing the claim for the part with
n<0
. Let
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}
, with
p_{0}
,
p_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
. We start by assuming that the region
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
has nonempty intersection with the set of convergence of the spherical Laurent series, so that we can write
Q_{p}^{-n}(q)
as in (
6.25
). By applying the second Fueter map to (
6.25
) and by using (
8.9
) we get

(8.22)

\displaystyle\Delta Q_{p}^{-n}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\Delta(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}.

By using (
8.9
) we get

(8.23)

\displaystyle\Delta\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}I.

Formula (
8.20
) follows by adding (
8.22
) and (
8.23
).

We now suppose that the region
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
has a nonempty intersection with the domain of convergence of the spherical Laurent series. The Taylor part
A_{T}(q,p)
follows by Theorem
7.20
, so we focus on the remaining part. Using the hypothesis, we can write
Q_{p}^{-n}(q)
as in (
6.28
).
Thus, by applying the second Fueter map and by using (
7.5
) we get

(8.24)

\displaystyle\Delta Q_{p}^{-n}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\Delta(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k)(2k-1)\mathcal{Q}_{2(k-1)}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4n\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k-1}(2k-1)\mathcal{Q}_{2(k-1)}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)},

and

(8.25)

\displaystyle\Delta\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k+1)(2k)\mathcal{Q}_{2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k)(2k-1)\mathcal{Q}_{2(k-1)}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)+1}I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4n\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k-1}(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle+4n\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k-1}(2k-1)\mathcal{Q}_{2(k-1)}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)+1}I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}(2k+3)\mathcal{Q}_{2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}

\displaystyle-4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)-1}I.

Finally, the series (
8.21
) follows by summing (
8.24
) and (
8.25
) and this completes the proof.
∎

Remark 8.15
.

Taking
p=0
in (
8.20
), by Remark
7.21
we have

A_{T}(q,0)=-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+2)(n+1)\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)a_{n+2}.

The other series in (
8.20
) when
p=0
has the nonzero terms only when
k=n
, and thus we have

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{2n}(q)|q|^{-4n-2}a_{-2n}-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{2n-1}(q)|q|^{-4n}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{2n}(q)|q|^{-2(2n+1)}a_{-2n}-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{2n-1}(q)|q|^{-2(2n-1+1)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{n}(q)|q|^{-2(n+1)}a_{-n}.

Moreover, for
p=0
the condition
p_{1}<|q-p_{0}|
is trivially satisfied and so we get to the Fueter regular Laurent series, where it is convergent, at the origin:

\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)=-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+2)(n+1)\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)a_{n+2}-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}S_{n}(q)|q|^{-2(n+1)}a_{-n}.

We now show that the Laurent Fueter regular spherical series assumes a special form if we consider a nonreal quaternion
q
.
Now, we describe the form of the Laurent–Fueter regular spherical series at
p
, which is valid outside the real axis.

Proposition 8.16
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in an axially symmetric open set
\Omega
that admits a spherical Laurent expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
as in (
2.39
), with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, the expansion being convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then, for
q\in U(p,R_{1},R_{2})\setminus\mathbb{R}
with
|q-p_{0}|\not=|p_{1}|
, we can write the Laurent Fueter regular spherical series as

\displaystyle\breve{f}(q)=\Delta f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(q)\left(a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-2}\left[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(q)\left(a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\left[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})\left(a_{2n}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1}\right)\right].

Proof.

We first focus on the Taylor part of
\Delta f(q)
, which we denote by
A_{T}(q,p)
. By Proposition
8.14
, we have

(8.26)

\displaystyle A_{T}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-2}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1}\right).

We now consider the two series in (
8.20
) and (
8.21
). Since these series converge in different regions, we split the proof into two cases. Let
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}
. In the remainder of the proof, we assume that the regions
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
and
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
have a nonempty intersection with the domain of convergence of the spherical Laurent series.

Case I:
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
. Our goal is to find a closed expression of the summations in (
8.20
) expressing
\breve{f}(q)
.

We start from the first series in (
8.20
). By using (
8.10
) and (
6.25
) we get

(8.27)

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[-4(n+k)(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2n+2k+1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(\underline{q})^{-1}|q-p_{0}|^{2}\left((q-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}-(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}\right)\right]|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(-2(n+k))(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)-1}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}-(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}\right]p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right)a_{-2n}-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}.

Now we focus on the second series in (
8.20
). By using (
8.10
) and (
6.25
) we obtain

(8.28)

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})|(q-p_{0})|^{-4(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[-2(2(n+k)-1)(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(\underline{q})^{-1}|q-p_{0}|^{2}\left((q-p_{0})^{2(n+k)-1}-(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)-1}\right)\right]|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left((-2(n+k)+1)(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}\right)p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}-(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}\right]p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right)a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p_{0})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}.

A compact expression of the third series in (
8.20
) follows by (
8.27
):

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}S_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}Ia_{-2n+1}

(8.29)

\displaystyle=-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right)p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}.

Hence the final result follows by putting together (
8.27
), (
8.28
) and (
8.29
).

Case II:
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
. Now, we aim to find a closed expression of (
8.21
).

First we find a compact expression of the first series in (
8.21
). By Corollary
7.5
and (
6.28
) we have

\displaystyle 4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=4(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+4(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{2(2k+2)}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}-(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{k+1}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k+2}p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}-(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}-(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k}-(q-p_{0})^{2k}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right)a_{-2n}

(8.30)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\quad-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}.

Now we focus on the second summation of (
8.21
). By Corollary
7.5
and (
6.25
) we get

(8.31)

\displaystyle 4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+3)\mathcal{Q}_{2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{k+1}(2k+3)(q-p_{0})^{2k+2}p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle\,\,+(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k+3}-(q-p_{0})^{2k+3}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+1+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k+3}p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle\,\,+(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k+3}-(q-p_{0})^{2k+3}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle\,\,-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k+1}-(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right)a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p_{0})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}.

Finally we focus on the third series of (
8.21
). By (
8.30
) we have

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)-1}Ia_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n(2k+1)\mathcal{Q}_{2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}

(8.32)

\displaystyle=2(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right)p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}+(\underline{q})^{-2}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}.

Hence the result follows by putting together (
8.26
), (
8.30
), (
8.31
) and (
8.32
).

∎

9.
Taylor series: axially polyanalytic functions of order 2

In this section we complete the study of the function spaces that appear in the quaternionic fine structures. The conjugate Fueter operator applied to a slice hyperholomorphic function gives rise to an axially polyanalytic function of order 2. The goal of this section is to study the series expansions of these functions. As in the preceding cases, we shall exploit the
*
-Taylor expansion and the spherical series of a slice hyperholomorphic function to obtain series expansions for axially polyanalytic functions of order 2.

9.1.
Polyanalytic regular series of order 2

The first type of series expansion that we consider in this section is the
*
-Taylor series.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
U\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be an axially symmetric open set. Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function (according to Definition
2.4
) admitting a
*
-Taylor expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p
convergent in a set contained in
U
. Then we say that the axially polyanalytic function of order
2
given by
h=\bar{D}f
has a polyanalytic regular series of order
2
at
p
.

To write the polyanalytic regular series of order 2 of
h=\bar{D}f
as above, we need to compute the action of the conjugate Fueter operator applied to the building block of the
*
-Taylor expansion at the point
p
, namely
(q-p)^{n*_{q,L}}
. As we did previously, below we will denote all
*_{q,L}
-products simply by
*
.
.

Proposition 9.2
.

Let
n\geq 1
and
q
,
p\in\mathbb{H}
and let
\bar{D}
be the conjugate Fueter operator in the variable
q
. Then

\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}=2\left(n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right).

Proof.

We start by applying the derivative
\partial_{q_{0}}
to
(q-p)^{*n}
, so that by (
2.10
) we get

(9.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}q^{k}p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}kq^{k-1}p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n-1}{k-1}q^{k-1}p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}q^{k}p^{n-k-1}(-1)^{n-k-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}.

By Lemma
5.2
we have

(\partial_{q_{0}}+\partial_{\underline{q}})(q-p)^{*n}=D(q-p)^{*n}=-2\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}.

This formula, together with (
9.1
) imply that

(9.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{\underline{q}}(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{*n}-2\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}-2\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}.

Finally, by (
9.1
) and (
9.2
) we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\partial_{q_{0}}-\partial_{\underline{q}})(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right).

This concludes the proof.
∎

The previous result can be written using a more compact notation, namely

(9.3)

\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}=2n\widetilde{P}_{2,n-1}(q,p),

where we set

(9.4)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p):=(q-p)^{*n}+\frac{1}{n+1}\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}(q-p)^{*(n+1-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}.

The main properties of the functions
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
are summarized in the following result.

Proposition 9.3
.

Let
p
,
q\in\mathbb{H}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. The functions
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
satisfy the following properties:

1)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
are right slice hyperholomorphic functions in
p
and left axially polyanalytic of order 2 in the variable
q
.

2)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
are related to the axially harmonic polynomials
\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p)
, see (
5.2
), by the formula

(9.5)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)=(q-p)^{*n}+\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p).

3)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
are left slice polyanalytic of order
n+1
in the variable
q
.

4)

For
q\notin\mathbb{R}
the functions
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
rewrite in the form

(9.6)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)=(q-p)^{*n}-\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{2(n+1)}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}-(q-p)^{*(n+1)}\right].

Proof.

1)

By formula (
9.4
), is clear that
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
is right slice hyperholomorphic in
p
, see Proposition
2.10
. By the Fueter theorem we have that

\displaystyle D^{2}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle D^{2}\left(\frac{\bar{D}(q-p)^{*(n+1)}}{2(n+1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Delta D(q-p)^{*(n+1)}}{2(n+1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

2)

Formula (
9.5
) follows by (
5.2
), the definition of the functions
\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p)
and the definition of
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
, see (
9.4
).

3)

By the second point of Proposition
5.4
, we have that the polynomials
\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p)
are left slice polyanalytic of order
n+1
in
q
. Hence we get the result by (
9.5
) and the fact that the sum of the left slice hyperholomorphic function
(q-p)^{*n}
and a left slice polyanalytic function of order
n+1
is a left slice polyanalytic function of order
n+1
.

4)

Formula (
9.6
) follows by combining (
9.5
) and (
5.5
).

∎

We know that any axially polyanalytic function of order
n
can be decomposed in terms of Fueter regular functions, see Theorem
2.66
. In the next result we show that the functions
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)
can be decomposed in terms of the regular Fueter polynomials
\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)
introduced in (
7.10
).

Proposition 9.4
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for
q
,
p\in\mathbb{H}
we have

(9.7)

\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)=(n+2)\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)+n\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)p-q_{0}n\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p).

Proof.

We start by observing that

(9.8)

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}-q_{0}\Delta(q-p)^{*n}\right]+q_{0}\Delta(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle h_{0}(q,p)+q_{0}h_{1}(q,p).

Proposition
9.2
and Theorem
7.7
yield

\displaystyle h_{0}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}-q_{0}\Delta(q-p)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2q_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right].

Since
2q_{0}=q+\bar{q}
we have

(9.9)

\displaystyle h_{0}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*q*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*\bar{q}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right].

By adding and subtracting the same quantity we rewrite (
9.9
) as

\displaystyle h_{0}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*p*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}p\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}(n-k+1)(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}p\right].

Changing index in the second series and using Theorem
7.7
we get

(9.10)

\displaystyle h_{0}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}(n-k+1)(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n}(n-k+1)(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}p\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}(n-k+1)(q-p)^{*(n-k)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(n-k)(q-p)^{*(n-k-1)}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(k-1)}p\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Delta\left((q-p)^{*(n+1)}\right)-\Delta\left((q-p)^{*n}\right)p.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2n(n+1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)+2n(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p)p.

For the term
h_{1}(q,p)
in (
9.8
), we can directly use Theorem
7.7
and we obtain

(9.11)

h_{1}(q,p)=\Delta\left((q-p)^{*n}\right)=-2n(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p).

By plugging (
9.10
) and (
9.11
) into (
9.8
) we can write

\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}=2n\left[(n+1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)+(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p)p-q_{0}(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p)\right].

Finally by (
9.3
) we arrive to the following:

(9.12)

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{2,n-1}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}}{2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(n+1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)+(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p)p

\displaystyle-q_{0}(n-1)\widetilde{Q}_{n-2}(q,p).

The result follows by rearranging the indices in (
9.12
).
∎

Remark 9.5
.

If we consider
p=0
in (
9.7
) we get back to (
4.11
).

By means of Proposition
9.4
and formula (
9.5
) we can write the harmonic functions
\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p)
in terms of the Fueter regular polynomials
\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)
. Precisely, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\widetilde{H}_{n}(q,p)=(n+2)\widetilde{Q}_{n}(q,p)+n\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)p-q_{0}n\widetilde{Q}_{n-1}(q,p)-(q-p)^{*n}.

Theorem 9.6
.

Let
U
be an axially symmetric open set in
\mathbb{H}
and let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in
U
that admits the
*
-Taylor expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p\in U

(9.13)

f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n},

convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R)\subseteq U
,
where
\frac{1}{R}=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}
. Then

(9.14)

\bar{D}f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n},\qquad q\in\Omega(p,R),\quad b_{n}:=2(n+1)a_{n+1}.

Proof.

We apply the operator
\bar{D}
to the function (
9.13
). By (
9.3
) we can write

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\widetilde{P}_{2,n-1}(q,p)a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n},

where
b_{n}:=2(n+1)a_{n+1}
. We prove the convergence of the series in (
9.14
).
If we assume that
q\in\mathbb{R}
the result is trivial, so we suppose that
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. Since
(q-p)^{*(n+1)}
is a slice hyperholomorphic function, by the representation formula for axially polyanalytic functions of order
2
, see Theorem
4.10
and (
9.3
), we have for
n\geq 1
:

\displaystyle|2(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\bar{D}(q-p)^{*(n+1)}a_{n+1}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\biggl|(n+1)\left[(q_{I}-p)^{n}+(q_{-I}-p)^{n}\right]a_{n+1}

\displaystyle+(n+1)I_{\underline{q}}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{(n)}-(q_{I}-p)^{n}\right]a_{n+1}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}I_{\underline{q}}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{(n+1)}-(q_{I}-p)^{(n+1)}\right]a_{n+1}\biggl|,

where if
p\in\mathbb{C}_{I}
, we take
q_{\pm I}=x\pm yI
. So we get

(9.15)

\displaystyle|2(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(n+1)\left[\left|(q_{I}-p)^{n}a_{n+1}\right|+\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{n}a_{n+1}\right|\right]

\displaystyle+|\underline{q}|^{-1}\left[\left|(q_{I}-p)^{n+1}a_{n+1}\right|+\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{n+1}a_{n+1}\right|\right].

Since
q\in\Omega(p,R)
, by the hypothesis on the coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
we get that the series

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{n}a_{n+1}\right|\quad\hbox{and}\quad\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{n+1}a_{n+1}\right|,

are convergent, therefore the series in (
9.14
) is convergent.

∎

Remark 9.7
.

If we consider
p=0
in (
9.14
) we get

(9.16)

\bar{D}f(q)=2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)P_{2,n}(q)a_{n+1},

since
\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,0)=P_{2,n}(q)
. Formula (
9.16
) is the Taylor expansion in terms of
P_{2,n}
in a neighbourhood of the origin of
\bar{D}f
.

Another useful way of writing the polyanalytic regular series is by means of the Clifford-Appell polynomials.

Proposition 9.8
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in a neighbourhood of
p\in\mathbb{H}
that admits a
*
-Taylor expansion as in (
9.13
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R)
, where
\frac{1}{R}=\limsup_{n}|a_{n}|^{1/n}
. Then the polyanalytic regular series of order
2
, for
q\in\Omega(p,R)
, can be written as

(9.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(k+2)\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-q_{0}\sum_{k=1}^{n}k\binom{n}{k}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}\right)a_{n+1},

Moreover if
q\notin\mathbb{R}
we have

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}=2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-\underline{q}^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}a_{n}-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right).

Proof.

By formula (
2.10
) and formula (
4.9
) we have

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}2k\mathcal{P}_{2,k-1}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n-1}{k-1}n\mathcal{P}_{2,k-1}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}n\mathcal{P}_{2,k}(q)p^{n-k-1}(-1)^{n-k-1}a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(n+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,k}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}a_{n+1}.

From the polyanalytic decomposition of the polynomials
\mathcal{P}_{2,k}(q)
, see (
4.11
) we deduce

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(k+2)\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}a_{n+1}

\displaystyle-q_{0}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(n+1)\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}k\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)p^{n-k}(-1)^{n-k}a_{n+1}.

Hence the result follows by Theorem
9.6
.
Now, we consider
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. By formula (
9.6
) we get

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)(q-p)^{*n}a_{n+1}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}-(q-p)^{*(n+1)}\right]a_{n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n(q-p)^{*(n-1)}a_{n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}a_{n}-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right),

which ends the proof.
∎

In the example below we expand the polyanalytic kernel
P^{L}_{2}(p,q)
as polyanalytic regular series of order 2.

Example 9.9
.

The polyanalytic kernel appearing in the integral representation of the axially polyanalytic functions of order
2
, see (
2.58
), has the following expansion

(9.18)

P_{2}^{L}(p,q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p+1)a_{n},\qquad q\in\Omega(p+1,1),

where
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}:=\{-2(-1)^{n}(n+1)\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
. Indeed, we apply the conjugate Cauchy-Fueter operator to (
2.16
), with respect to the variable
q
. By (
9.3
) we have

\displaystyle\bar{D}S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n+1}\bar{D}(q-p-1)^{*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}n\widetilde{P}_{2,n-1}(q,p+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p+1).

Since
\bar{D}S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)=P_{2}^{L}(p,q)
, see (
2.58
), we get (
9.18
).

9.2.
Polyanalytic spherical series of order 2

Another possible way to get a series expansion for an axially polyanalytic function of order
2
is to consider spherical series which allow us to have convergence in suitable Euclidean neighbourhoods. Thus we introduce the following notion:

Definition 9.10
.

Let
U
be an axially symmetric open set in
\mathbb{H}
. Let
f
be a function slice hyperholomorphic in
U
admitting a spherical series expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p\in U
convergent in a convenient Euclidean open set contained in
U
. Then we say that
\bar{D}f
has a polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
.

Using the spherical expansion of the slice hyperholomorphic function
f
, we can write a compact formula for the polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
.

Theorem 9.11
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in a neighborhood of
p\in\mathbb{H}
containing
U(p,R)
for some
R>0
, where it admits a spherical expansion (
2.21
), with coefficients

\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R)
. Let
h
be the axially polyanalytic function of order 2 in a neighborhood of
p
given by
h=\overline{D}f
. Then,
h
has the following polyanalytic spherical expansion of order 2

(9.19)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)(q-p_{0})\,Q^{n}_{p}(q)\left[a_{2n+2}+(q-p)a_{2n+3}\right]

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q_{0}-p_{0})\,\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)\left[a_{2n+2}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+3}\right]+4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q^{n}_{p}(q)\,a_{2n+1},

where the function
\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)
is defined in (
5.14
).

Proof.

We apply
\bar{D}
term by term to (
2.21
). The exchange between
\bar{D}
and the summation
is justified as in Theorem
5.13
. We start proving the result for
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. By (
4.23
), applied first to
Q_{p}^{n}(q)
, we have

(9.20)

\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)\right)=4n(q-p_{0})Q_{p}^{n-1}(q)+4(q_{0}-p_{0})\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p),

where we have used (
5.15
) and (
5.27
). Similarly, applying (
4.23
) to
Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)
and using again (
5.15
), we obtain

(9.21)

\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)\right)=4n(q-p_{0})Q_{p}^{n-1}(q)(q-p)+4(q_{0}-p_{0})\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)(\bar{q}-p)+4Q_{p}^{n}(q).

Therefore, applying
\bar{D}
term by term to (
2.21
)
and substituting (
9.20
) and (
9.21
), and changing the indexes we get (
9.19
). It remains to consider the real points. Let

q=q_{0}\in U(p,R)\cap\mathbb{R}
. Since
Q_{p}(q_{0})\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathcal{L}_{n}(q_{0},p)=nQ_{p}^{n-1}(q_{0}).

By (
4.15
) we deduce that
\overline{D}(\cdot))=4\partial_{q_{0}}(\cdot)
. Since
Q_{p}(q)
and
Q_{p}(q)(q-p)
are slice hyperholomorphic,by (
5.27
) we have

(9.22)

\bar{D}[Q_{p}^{n}(q_{0})]=8n(q_{0}-p_{0})Q_{p}^{n-1}(q_{0}),

and

(9.23)

\bar{D}[Q_{p}^{n}(q_{0})(q_{0}-p)]=8n(q_{0}-p_{0})Q_{p}^{n-1}(q_{0})(q_{0}-p)+4Q_{p}^{n}(q_{0}).

Therefore, the right-hand side of (
9.19
) coincides with the series obtained by applying
\bar{D}
term by term to (
2.21
) at the real points.
∎

Now we show that the polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
has the same convergence set as that of its ”primitive” function
f
and of the spherical series, and so the same radius of convergence of the harmonic and of the regular series.

Proposition 9.12
.

With the notations in Theorem
9.11
, let
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be such that

\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}=\frac{1}{R}.

The polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
converges (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R)

for
p\in\mathbb{H}
.

Proof.

Let
K
be a compact subset of
U(p,R)
. If
q\in K
then
|Q_{p}^{n}(q)|\leq r^{2n}
for some
r
such that
0<r<R
. Fix
R^{\prime}
with
r<R^{\prime}<R
so that
|a_{n}|\leq(1/R^{\prime})^{n}
for
n
sufficiently large. By Theorem
9.11
we know that a spherical polyanalytic series of order
2
can be decomposed in two parts. We start by estimating the terms of the second series in (
9.19
). By Lemma
5.15
we have

\displaystyle 4\left|\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)\right|\left|q_{0}-p_{0}\right|\left(\left|a_{2n+2}\right|+\left|\bar{q}-p\right|\left|a_{2n+3}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4(n+1)}{R^{\prime 3}}\left(\frac{r}{R^{\prime}}\right)^{2n}\sqrt{r^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left[R^{\prime}+\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}.

Hence the second series of (
9.19
) is dominated by the series
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\alpha_{n}
. We note that here we used
|\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)|\leq(n+1)r^{2n}
, see (
5.14
),
and
|\bar{q}-p|\leq\sqrt{r^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}
by (
5.32
).

We now focus on the first summation in (
9.19
). By Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
we get

\displaystyle 4\left|(n+1)(q-p_{0})Q_{p}^{n}(q)[a_{2n+2}+(q-p)a_{2n+3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4(n+1)}{R^{\prime 3}}\left(\frac{r}{R^{\prime}}\right)^{2n}\sqrt{r^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left[R^{\prime}+\left(\sqrt{r^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle:=\beta_{n}.

Hence the first part of the expansion is dominated by the convergent series
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\beta_{n}
.
Finally the third series in (
9.19
) can be estimated by
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{n}
, where
\gamma_{n}:=\frac{4}{R^{\prime}}\left(\frac{r}{R^{\prime}}\right)^{2n}
.

Thus we deduce that the polyanalytic spherical series is dominated by

|\bar{D}f(q)|\leq\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\alpha_{n}+\beta_{n}+\gamma_{n}).

Since
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\alpha_{n}+\beta_{n}+\gamma_{n})
is convergent, by the ratio test, we get the result.
∎

We can write the polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
in terms of the polyanalytic functions
\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)
defined in (
4.10
).

Theorem 9.13
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in an axially symmetric open set
U
. Let us assume that
f
admits a spherical series at an arbitrarily fixed point
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in U
as in (
2.21
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R)
,
R>0
. Then the polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
, for
q\in U(p,R)
, can be written as

(9.24)

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left[\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}(2n)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k-1)}a_{2n}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)}a_{2n+1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}(2n)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)-1}Ia_{2n+1}\right].

Proof.

By the binomial theorem and formula (
4.9
) we have

(9.25)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}2k\mathcal{P}_{2,2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}(2n)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k-1)}.

Now, we focus on the second part of the spherical series. By (
4.9
) we can write

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)+1}I\right)

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)}

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,-2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(2k)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)+1}I

\displaystyle=2\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)}

(9.26)

\displaystyle\,\,\,\,\,-2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{k}(2n)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2(n-k)-1}I.

By putting together (
9.25
) and (
9.26
) we get the result.

∎

Remark 9.14
.

Setting
p=0
in (
9.24
), we observe that the nonzero terms correspond to
k=n-1
, and thus we obtain

(9.27)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(2n)\mathcal{P}_{2,2n-1}(q)a_{2n}+2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(2n+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2n}(q)a_{2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)a_{n+1}.

Formula (
9.27
) is the Taylor expansion of a function
h\in\mathcal{AP}_{2}^{L}(U)
, where
U
is an axially symmetric domain containing the origin. We observe that the same result was obtained in Remark
9.7
by taking
p=0
in the polyanalytic regular series of order
2
.

In the next result we show an alternative way of writing a spherical polyanalytic series of
order 2.

Proposition 9.15
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in an axially symmetric open set
U
that admits a spherical series at an arbitrarily fixed point
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in U
as in (
2.21
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R)
,
R>0
. Then for
q\in U(p,R)\setminus\mathbb{R}
we can write the polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
as

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right]

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1})\right).

Proof.

By using (
2.10
) and formula (
4.13
) we get

(9.28)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right]^{n}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left[(q-p_{0})^{2}+p_{1}^{2}\right]^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left[Q_{p}^{n}(q)\right]-\underline{q}^{-1}\left[Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{n}(q)\right].

By using similar arguments for the second part of spherical series we have

(9.29)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}-\sum_{k=1}^{n}\binom{n}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)+1}I\right)

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)+1}I\right)+

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{2(n-k)}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{2(n-k)+1}I\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)\right)-\underline{q}^{-1}\left[Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p)-Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)\right].

The result follows by putting together (
9.28
) and (
9.29
).
∎

We note that, similarly to the cases of axially harmonic and regular functions, the connection between the truncated polyanalytic regular and spherical series of order 2 can also be expressed using the right global operator.

Theorem 9.16
.

Let
p\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathbb{R}
,
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be a given sequence. Define the sequence
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
by

b_{n-1}=-2n\left(c_{n}a_{2n}+c_{n-1}a_{2n-1}\right),\qquad n\geq 1,

where
c_{n}:=2^{n}n!(-1)^{n}
. Then we have the following connection between the truncated polyanalytic regular series and the truncated polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
:

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{N}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=0}^{N}V_{p}^{n+1}\left[\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})+(n+1)Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{2n+2}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}V_{p}^{n+1}\left[\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})(\bar{q}-p)+(n+1)Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p_{0})(q-p)\right]a_{2n+3}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=0}^{N}V_{p}^{n}(Q_{p}^{n}(q))a_{2n+1}

where
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
is fixed.

Proof.

The result follows by using arguments similar to those used to prove Theorem
5.20
.
∎

10.
Laurent series: axially polyanalytic functions of order
2

In this section we complete the study by investigating the Laurent series of
h=\bar{D}f
, where
f
is a slice hyperholomorphic function which is expanded in series at a point
p\in\mathbb{H}
.

10.1.
Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order 2

We now introduce and study a series around a generic quaternion
p
, which we call Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
. It is based on the application of the operator
\bar{D}
to a slice hyperholomorphic function expanded as a
*
-Laurent series.

Definition 10.1
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be an axially symmetric open set and let
f
be a function slice hyperholomorphic in
\Omega
that admits a
*
-Laurent expansion at
p\in\mathbb{H}
convergent in a set contained in
\Omega
. We say that
h=\bar{D}f
has a Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order 2 at
p
.

To study these series we compute
\bar{D}(q-p)^{-n*_{q,L}}
. As customary in this work, we write
*
to denote the
*_{q,L}
-products.

Theorem 10.2
.

Let
q,p\in\mathbb{H}
satisfy
q\notin[p]
, and let
\bar{D}
denote the conjugate Fueter operator with respect to
q
. Then for
n\geq 1
we have

\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}=-2\left(n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k)}*(q-p)^{*(k+1)}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q).

Proof.

We start by observing that
D+\bar{D}=2\partial_{q_{0}}
, so by Theorem
6.2
we have

(10.1)

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{-*n}-D(q-p)^{-*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2n(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}-2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k-1)}*(q-p)^{*k}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2n(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}-2\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k)}*(q-p)^{*(k+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q).

By Proposition
6.9
we get that

\left[(q-p)^{*(n+1)}*(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)=1,

which implies

(10.2)

(q-p)^{-*(n+1)}=(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q).

Thus, by plugging (
10.2
) in (
10.1
) we have

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)-2\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k)}*(q-p)^{*(k+1)}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\left(n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k)}*(q-p)^{*(k+1)}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q),

which proves the result.
∎

The above formula can be rewritten as

(10.3)

\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}=-2\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q),

where

(10.4)

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p):=n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n-k)}*(q-p)^{*(k+1)}.

The functions
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
defined above possess the properties described in the next result.

Proposition 10.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
p
,
q\in\mathbb{H}
such that
q\notin[p]
. Then we have

1)

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
satisfy

(10.5)

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)=n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\mathcal{H}_{n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q),

where
\mathcal{H}_{n}(q,p)
are defined in (
6.2
).

2)

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
satisfy

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)=-2\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)p-2\mathcal{M}_{n-1}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)+2q_{0}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p),

where
\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)
are defined in (
8.3
).

3)

The function
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)
is axially polyanalytic of order
2
.

4)

The functions
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
are left slice polyanalytic of order
n+2
in
q
, and are right slice hyperholomorphic in the variable
p
.

5)

For
q\notin\mathbb{R}
we have

(10.6)

\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)=n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}+\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}}{2}\left[(q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)

Proof.

1)

It follows by (
6.2
) and by the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q)=(q-p)*(\bar{q}-p)
.

2)

By (
10.3
), (
2.28
) and (
2.58
) we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{2(n-1)!}\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\bar{D}S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{2(n-1)!}\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}P_{2}^{L}(p,q)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q).

Now by using the expression of the polyanalytic kernel
P_{2}^{L}(p,q)
and the Leibniz rule we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{2(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}[-F_{L}(p,q)p]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)+\frac{(-1)^{n}}{2(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\left[q_{0}F_{L}(p,q)\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{2(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}[-F_{L}(p,q)]p\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)+\frac{(-1)^{n}}{2(n-1)!}\left[q_{0}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(n-1)\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-2}F_{L}(p,q)\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q).

Since the
F
-kernel
F_{L}(p,q)
is axially Fueter regular in
q
we can replace
\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}
with
\frac{\bar{D}^{n-1}}{2^{n-1}}
. Thus by formula (
8.4
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!2^{n}}\left(\bar{D}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)p+\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!2^{n}}q_{0}\left(\bar{D}^{n-1}F_{L}(p,q)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)

\displaystyle+\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{2^{n-1}(n-2)!}\left(\bar{D}^{n-2}F_{L}(p,q)\right)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{n+1}(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)p+2q_{0}\mathcal{M}_{n}(q,p)-2\mathcal{M}_{n-1}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(q),

as stated.

3)

Since for
q\notin[p]
the function
(q-p)^{-*n}
is slice hyperholomorphic by (
10.3
) and the Fueter mapping theorem we get that the function
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)
is axially polyanalytic of order 2.

4)

By (
10.5
) and reasoning as in the proof of point 2) of Proposition
5.4
we get that the functions
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
are left slice polyanalytic of order
n+2
. By formula (
10.5
) and point 2) of Proposition
6.3
, we deduce that the functions
\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)
are right slice hyperholomorphic in
p
.

5)

Formula (
10.6
) follows by combining formula (
6.3
) and (
10.5
).

∎

Remark 10.4
.

If we set
p=0
in (
10.3
), we obtain

(10.7)

\bar{D}(q^{-n})=-2R_{n}(q)|q|^{-2(n+1)},\qquad n\in\mathbb{N},

where the polynomials
R_{n}(q)
are given by

(10.8)

R_{n}(q)=\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,0)=n\bar{q}^{n+1}+\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\bar{q}^{n-i}q^{i+1}.

Furthermore, if
q\in\mathbb{H}\setminus\mathbb{R}
, by (
10.6
) with
p=0
, we have

(10.9)

R_{n}(q)=n\bar{q}^{n+1}+\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}|q|^{2}}{2}(q^{n}-\bar{q}^{n}).

The Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
can be written in a more compact way.

Theorem 10.5
.

Let
\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{H}
be an axially symmetric open set and let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in
\Omega
that admits
*
-Laurent expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p\in\mathbb{H}

(10.10)

f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n},\qquad\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subset\mathbb{H},

convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})\subset\Omega

where
\frac{1}{R_{2}}=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}
and
R_{1}=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{-n}|^{\frac{1}{n}}
. Then we can write the Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
as

(10.11)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)c_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)c_{-n},

where
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
,
\{c_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{2(n+1)a_{n+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{c_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{-2a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Proof.

By formulas (
4.9
) and (
10.3
) we have

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)c_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)c_{-n}.

The convergence of the first series follows by Theorem
9.6
. We focus on the second series. If
q\in\mathbb{R}
the result is trivial. If we consider
q\notin\mathbb{R}
, by formula (
10.3
) and Theorem
4.10
, and taking
q_{\pm I}\in[q]
if
p\in\mathbb{C}_{I}
, we get

\displaystyle-2\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{D}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-n\left[(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}+(q_{-I}-p)^{-(n+1)}\right]a_{-n}

\displaystyle-nI_{\underline{q}}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{-(n+1)}-(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}\right]a_{-n}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}I_{\underline{q}}I\left[(q_{-I}-p)^{-n}-(q_{I}-p)^{-n}\right]a_{-n}.

So we have

\displaystyle|2\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2n\left[\left|(q_{I}-p)^{-(n+1)}a_{-n}\right|+\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{-(n+1)}a_{-n}\right|\right]

\displaystyle+|\underline{q}|^{-1}\left[\left|(q_{I}-p)^{-n}a_{-n}\right|+\left|(q_{-I}-p)^{-n}a_{-n}\right|\right].

Since
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
we get that the series

\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{-(n+1)}a_{-n}\right|,\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left|(q_{\pm I}-p)^{-n}a_{-n}\right|,

are convergent. So we have that the series in (
10.11
) is convergent where stated.

∎

Remark 10.6
.

If we consider
p=0
in (
10.11
), and using the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{c,0}(q)=|q|^{2}
we get the following expression of a polyanalytic Laurent series of order 2 in a neighbourhood of the origin:

(10.12)

\bar{D}f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)c_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|q|^{-2(n+1)}R_{n}(q)c_{-n},

where
\{c_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{2(n+1)a_{n+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{c_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{-2a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.
