Similarly to what happens in the case of Laurent series for Fueter regular or axially harmonic functions, also the Laurent polyanalytic regular series can be written in terms of the kernel of the integral representation of the axially polyanalytic function of order
2
.

Proposition 10.7
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function that admits a
*
-Laurent expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p
as in (
10.10
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
, with
R_{1}
,
R_{2}
as in Theorem
10.5
. Then for
q\in\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})\setminus\mathbb{R}
the Laurent polyanalytic series of order
2
, can be written as

(10.13)

h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}P_{2}^{L}(p,q)b_{-n}.

where
\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{2(n+1)a_{n+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{b_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
2.43
, formula (
2.58
), expression (
9.3
) and the fact that the conjugate Fueter operator commutes with
\partial_{q_{0}}
we get

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\bar{D}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\bar{D}S^{-1}_{L}(p,q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n\widetilde{P}_{2,n-1}(q,p)a_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}P_{2}^{L}(p,q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}P_{2}^{L}(p,q)a_{-n}.

Finally, by setting the coefficients
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
as in the statement we get the result.
∎

Remark 10.8
.

The Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order 2 can be written in terms of the slice hyperholomorphic Cauchy kernel by using Proposition
2.78
.

Moreover if we assume that
p=0
in (
10.13
) we obtain a Laurent polyanalytic series of order 2 in a neighbourhood of the origin in terms of the polyanalytic Cauchy kernel (see (
2.49
)):

\bar{D}f(q)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{4(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\left(q_{0}E(q)\right)b_{-n},

where
\{b_{n}\}_{n\geq 0}:=\{2(n+1)a_{n+1}\}_{n\geq 0}
and
\{b_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}:=\{a_{-n}\}_{n\geq 1}
.

In
[
52
]
the authors proved that the polyanalytic kernel can be written in terms of the polyanalytic polynomials
\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)
, see (
4.10
). Precisely, we have

(10.14)

P_{2}^{L}(p,q)=2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)p^{-2-n},\qquad|q|<|p|.

The above expansion and (
4.11
) pave the way to get a Laurent polyanalytic regular series in terms of the Clifford-Appell polynomials
\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)
.

Proposition 10.9
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function that admits a
*
-Laurent expansion centered at an arbitrarily fixed point
p
as in (
2.27
) whose coefficients are
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Assume moreover that the region
|q|<|p|
has nonempty intersection with
\Omega(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then, on that intersection, we can write the Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
as

(10.15)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}\binom{k}{n-1}(k+1)_{2}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2q_{0}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\binom{k-1}{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=2}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\binom{k-1}{n-2}(k)_{2}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}.

Moreover, if
q\notin\mathbb{R}
we can write the Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
as

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n}.

Proof.

We first assume that the region
|q|<|p|
has a nonempty intersection with the domain of convergence of the
*
-Laurent series. So, using the expansion of the Laurent polyanalytic regular series of order
2
proved in (
10.13
) and series expansion of the polyanalytic kernel, see (
10.14
), we have

(10.16)

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}P_{2}^{L}(p,q)b_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{k+1}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\mathcal{P}_{2,k}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}.

In order to show formula (
10.15
) we plug into (
10.16
) the polyanalytic decomposition of the polynomials
\mathcal{P}_{2,k}(q)
, see (
4.11
), and we obtain

(10.17)

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}\frac{(k+1)(k+2)}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}\frac{k(k+1)}{(n-1)!}\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\left(q_{0}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)\right)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}.

Since the Clifford-Appell polynomials are axially Fueter regular by formula (
2.46
) we get

(10.18)

\partial_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{n}(q)=n\mathcal{Q}_{n-1}(q),\qquad n\geq 1.

The Leibniz rule yields

(10.19)

\displaystyle\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\left(q_{0}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\binom{n-1}{\ell}\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1-\ell}q_{0}\right)\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{\ell}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{0}\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)\right)+\binom{n-1}{n-2}\left(\partial_{q_{0}}q_{0}\right)\left(\partial_{q_{0}}^{n-2}\mathcal{Q}_{k-1}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{0}\frac{(k-1)!}{(k-n)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n}(q)+(n-1)\frac{(k-1)!}{(k-n+1)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q).

Finally by plugging (
10.18
) and (
10.19
) into (
10.17
) we arrive at

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}\frac{(k+1)(k+2)k!}{(n-1)!(k-n+1)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2q_{0}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\frac{(k-1)!}{(n-1)!(k-n)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\frac{k(k+1)(k-1)!}{(n-2)!(k-n+1)!}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)b_{n}+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n-1}^{\infty}\binom{k}{n-1}(k+1)_{2}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2q_{0}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\binom{k-1}{n-1}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\binom{k-1}{n-2}(k)_{2}\mathcal{Q}_{k-n+1}(q)p^{-2-k}b_{-n}.

This proves formula (
10.15
). Now, we suppose that
q\notin\mathbb{R}
. By Theorem
10.5
and formula (
9.6
) we have that

(10.20)

2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\widetilde{P}_{2,n}(q,p)a_{n+1}=2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n}.

By (
10.6
) and Proposition
6.9
we have

(10.21)

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{R}_{2,n}(q,p)\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}n(\bar{q}-p)^{*(n+1)}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n-1}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[(q-p)^{*n}-(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}\right]\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-n}(q)a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{-*n}-(q-p)^{-*n}\right]a_{-n}.

Thus, by (
10.11
) and by combining (
10.20
) and (
10.21
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p)^{-*n}a_{-n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{-*n}-(q-p)^{-*n}\right]a_{-n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(q-p)^{*n}a_{n}\right)-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left[(\bar{q}-p)^{*n}-(q-p)^{*n}\right]a_{n},

which proves the last assertion.
∎

10.2.
Laurent polyanalytic spherical series

We now discuss a Laurent polyanalytic series of order
2
centered at a generic quaternion
p
that converges in an open Euclidean neighbourhood.

Definition 10.10
.

Let
\Omega
be an axially symmetric open set. Let
f
be a function slice hyperholomorphic in
\Omega
with a spherical Laurent expansion at
p
convergent in a subset of
\Omega
. Then we say that
h=\bar{D}f
has a Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
at an arbitrarily fixed point
p
.

By using the exact form of the Laurent spherical series we can provide a compact expression of the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
.

Theorem 10.11
.

Let
f
be a slice hyperholomorphic function in a neighbourhood of
p\in\mathbb{H}
containing the Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
, for some
0<R_{1}<R_{2}
, where it admits the spherical Laurent expansion (
2.39
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, converging (absolutely and uniformly on compact subsets) in
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Let
h=\bar{D}f
be the axially polyanalytic function of order
2
in a neighbourhood of
p
. Then
h
admits the following Laurent polyanalytic
spherical expansion of order
2
:

(10.22)

\begin{split}h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)=\ &4\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}n\,(q-p_{0})\,Q^{n-1}_{p}(q)\left[a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right]\\
&+4\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(q_{0}-p_{0})\,\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)\left[a_{2n}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1}\right]+4\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q^{n}_{p}(q)\,a_{2n+1},\end{split}

with
\mathcal{L}_{n}(q,p)
as in (
5.14
).

Proof.

Apply
\bar{D}
term by term to (
2.39
). The exchange between the summation and the operator follows as Theorem
6.12
. The expression (
10.22
) follows by (
9.20
), (
9.21
). It remains to consider the real points. Let

q=q_{0}\in U(p,R_{1},R_{2})\cap\mathbb{R}
. Since
Q_{p}(q_{0})\in\mathbb{R}
, we have

\mathcal{L}_{n}(q_{0},p)=nQ_{p}^{n-1}(q_{0}),\qquad n\in\mathbb{Z}.

By (
9.22
) and (
9.23
), which remain valid for every
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, the right-hand side of (
10.22
) coincides precisely with the series obtained by applying
\bar{D}
term by term to the Laurent spherical expansion (
2.39
).
∎

We now show that the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
has the same convergence set as the slice hyperholomorphic Laurent spherical series.

Proposition 10.12
.

With the notations in Theorem
10.11
let
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, set

R_{1}:=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{-n}|^{\frac{1}{n}},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\frac{1}{R_{2}}:=\limsup_{n\to\infty}|a_{n}|^{\frac{1}{n}},

with
R_{1}<R_{2}
. The Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
converges (absolutely and uniformly on compact subsets) in the Cassini shell:

U(p,R_{1},R_{2}):=\{q\in\mathbb{H}\,:\,R_{1}^{2}<|(q-p_{0})^{2}+p_{1}^{2}|<R_{2}^{2}\},

where
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
,
I\in\mathbb{S}
.

Proof.

Let
K
be a compact subset of the Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
. Then there exist
r_{1},r_{2}
such that
R_{1}<r_{1}<r_{2}<R_{2}
and
r_{1}^{2n}<|Q_{p}^{n}(q)|<r_{2}^{2n}
for
q\in K
. Choose
R^{\prime}
and
R^{\prime\prime}
such that
R_{1}<R^{\prime}<r_{1}<r_{2}<R^{\prime\prime}<R_{2}
. By the assumptions on the coefficients, we have
|a_{n}|\leq(1/R^{\prime\prime})^{n}
and
|a_{-n}|\leq(R^{\prime})^{n}
for all sufficiently large
n
. By Theorem
10.11
we can write the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
as

(10.23)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\mathcal{L}_{n+1}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})\left[a_{2n+2}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+3}\right]

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p_{0})\left[a_{2n+2}+(q-p)a_{2n+3}\right]+4\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n+1}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)(q_{0}-p_{0})\left[a_{-2n}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{-2n+1}\right]

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}nQ_{p}^{-n-1}(q)(q-p_{0})\left[a_{-2n}+(q-p)a_{-2n+1}\right]+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{-n}(q)a_{-2n+1}.

The convergence of the Taylor part of (
10.23
) follows by Proposition
9.12
, so we focus on the remaining part. By Lemma
5.14
and Lemma
5.15
we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)||q_{0}-p_{0}|\left[|a_{-2n}|+|\bar{q}-p||a_{-2n+1}|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{n}{r_{1}^{2}R^{\prime}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}}{r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\left(R^{\prime}+\left(p_{1}+\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}\right)\right):=A_{n},

where we used
|\mathcal{L}_{-n}(q,p)|\leq n|Q_{p}(q)|^{-n-1}
, see (
5.14
), and
|\bar{q}-p|\leq\sqrt{r_{2}^{2}+p_{1}^{2}}+p_{1}
, see (
5.32
).
Similarly, we have

n|Q_{p}^{-n-1}(q)||q-p_{0}|\left[|a_{-2n}|+|q-p||a_{-2n+1}|\right]\leq A_{n}.

and

|Q_{p}^{-n}(q)a_{-2n+1}|\leq\frac{1}{R^{\prime}}\left(\frac{R^{\prime}}{r_{1}}\right)^{2n}:=B_{n},

so, we have

|\bar{D}f(q)|\leq 4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(2A_{n}+B_{n}).

By the ratio test the series
\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(A_{n}+B_{n})
is convergent. This concludes the proof.
∎

In suitable subsets of
\mathbb{H}
we can write an expansion of the spherical Laurent polyanalytic expansion in terms of the functions
R_{n}(q)
defined in (
10.8
) and the polyanalytic functions
\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)
defined in (
4.10
).

Proposition 10.13
.

Let
q,p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
. We assume that
f
is a slice hyperholomorphic function that admits a spherical Laurent expansion at an arbitrarily fixed point
p
as in (
2.39
) with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
,
0<R_{1}<R_{2}
. Assume that the region
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
meets
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
; then, on that intersection, we can write the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
of
h=\bar{D}f
as

(10.24)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{T}(q,p)-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}R_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}Ia_{-2n+1}.

where
C_{T}(q,p)
is the Taylor part of the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
, given in formula (
9.24
). If instead the region
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
meets
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
, then on that intersection another representation of the Laurent spherical polyanalytic series of order
2
is given by

(10.25)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{T}(q,p)-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle+4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)+1}Ia_{-2n+1},

Proof.

We apply the operator
\bar{D}
to a function
f
expanded as in (
6.24
).
The expression of the Taylor part
C_{T}(q,p)
follows by Theorem
9.13
. Let
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}
. We first assume that the region
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
has a nonempty intersection with the domain of convergence of the spherical Laurent series. Hence, we may expand
Q_{p}^{-n}(q)
as in (
6.25
). Then, using (
10.7
), we obtain

(10.26)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\bar{D}(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}.

Similarly, by (
6.25
) and by using (
10.7
) we have

(10.27)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\bar{D}\left[(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}\right]p_{1}^{2k}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\bar{D}\left[(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}\right]p_{1}^{2k+1}I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}p_{1}^{2k}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}I.

Formula (
10.24
) follows by putting together (
10.26
) and (
10.27
).

Now, we assume that the intersection of the region
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
with the domain of convergence of the spherical Laurent is nonempty. The expression of the Taylor part follows by Theorem
9.13
, so we focus on applying the operator
\bar{D}
to the other part of (
6.24
). We can use the expansions of
Q_{p}^{-n}(q)
as in (
6.28
) and by (
9.6
) we have

(10.28)

\displaystyle\bar{D}(Q_{p}^{-n}(q))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\bar{D}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}k\mathcal{P}_{2,2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k-1}\mathcal{P}_{2,2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)},

and

(10.29)

\displaystyle\bar{D}\left(Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}k\mathcal{P}_{2,2k-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)+1}I

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}

\displaystyle+4n\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)-1}I

Formula (
10.25
) follows by putting together (
10.28
) and (
10.29
).
∎

Remark 10.14
.

If we take
p=0
in (
10.24
), by Remark
9.14
and (
4.11
) we have

C_{T}(q,0)=2\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(n+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,n}(q)a_{n+1}.\\

The only nonzero terms in the other series appearing in (
10.24
) correspond to
k=0
. Hence, they reduce to

-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}R_{2n}(q)|q|^{-4n-2}a_{-2n}-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}R_{2n-1}(q)|q|^{-4n}a_{-2n+1}=-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}R_{n}(q)|q|^{-2(n+1)}a_{-n}.

Thus we get back to the polyanalytic Laurent series of order
2
in a neighbourhood of the origin obtained in (
10.12
).

Proposition 10.15
.

Let
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}\in\mathbb{H}
be a fixed point. We assume that
f
is a slice hyperholomorphic function that admits a spherical Laurent expansion at
p
as in (
2.39
), with coefficients
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\subseteq\mathbb{H}
, convergent (absolutely and uniformly on the compact subsets) in a Cassini shell
U(p,R_{1},R_{2})
,
0<R_{1}<R_{2}
. Then, for every
q\in U(p,R_{1},R_{2})\setminus\mathbb{R}
with
|q-p_{0}|\not=|p_{1}|
, we can write the Laurent polyanalytic spherical series of order
2
as

(10.30)

\displaystyle h(q)=\bar{D}f(q)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(q)\left(a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(q)\left(a_{2n}+(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right)-\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})\left(a_{2n}+(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1}\right)\right).

Proof.

We first focus on the Taylor part of
\bar{D}f(q)
, which we denote by
C_{T}(q,p)
. By Proposition
9.15
, we have

(10.31)

\displaystyle C_{T}(q,p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right]

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(q)a_{2n}+Q_{p}^{n}(q)(q-p)a_{2n+1}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})a_{2n}-Q_{p}^{n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p)a_{2n+1}\right).

We now focus on the two series in (
10.24
) and (
10.25
). Since these series converge in different domains of convergence, we divide the proof into two cases. Let
p=p_{0}+Ip_{1}
. In the sequel of the proof, we assume that the regions
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
and
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
have a nonempty intersection with the domain of convergence of the spherical Laurent series.

Case I:
|p_{1}|<|q-p_{0}|
.

We focus on the first series of (
10.24
). By (
10.9
) and (
6.25
) we have

(10.32)

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}\left[2(n+k)(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)+1}+\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}|q-p_{0}|^{2}}{2}\left((q-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}-(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}\right)\right]p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(-2(n+k))(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)-1}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}-(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}\right]p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\partial_{q_{0}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}.

Now, we consider the second summation of (
10.24
). By (
10.9
) and (
6.25
) we have

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}R_{2(n+k)-1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)}\left[\left(2(n+k)-1\right)(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\frac{(\underline{q})^{-1}|q-p_{0}|^{2}}{2}\left((q-p_{0})^{2(n+k)-1}-(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(n+k)-1}\right)\right]a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)}(1-2(n+k))p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left((\bar{q}-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}-(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}\right)a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=2\partial_{q_{0}}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}p_{1}^{2k}a_{-2n+1}\right)

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left((\bar{q}-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}-(q-p_{0})^{-2(n+k)+1}\right)a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}

(10.33)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p_{0})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}.

Finally, we compute the third summation of (
10.24
). By (
10.32
) we have

(10.34)

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}R_{2(n+k)}(q-p_{0})|q-p_{0}|^{-4(n+k)-2}p_{1}^{2k+1}Ia_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}Q_{p}^{-n}(q)p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}.

Hence formula (
10.30
) follows by putting together (
10.31
), (
10.32
), (
10.33
) and (
10.34
).

Case II:
|q-p_{0}|<|p_{1}|
.

We consider the first summation of (
10.25
). By (
4.13
) and (
6.28
) we have

(10.35)

\displaystyle-4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{k+1}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}\frac{n}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}-(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle+(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k+1}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}-(q-p_{0})^{2(k+1)}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k}-(q-p_{0})^{2k}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]a_{-2n}.

The second summation of (
10.25
) can be dealt with using (
4.13
) and (
6.28
), so we have

(10.36)

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}(2k+1)\mathcal{P}_{2,2k}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\partial_{q_{0}}(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k-1}{k}\left[(\bar{q}-p_{0})^{2k+1}-(q-p_{0})^{2k+1}\right]p_{1}^{-2(n+k)}a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle-(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})(\bar{q}-p_{0})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)(q-p_{0})\right]a_{-2n+1}

Finally, we focus on the third summation of (
10.25
). By (
10.35
) we have

(10.37)

\displaystyle 4\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}\binom{n+k}{k}n\mathcal{P}_{2,2k+1}(q-p_{0})p_{1}^{-2(n+k+1)+1}Ia_{-2n+1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\partial_{q_{0}}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}+(\underline{q})^{-1}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\left[Q_{p}^{-n}(\bar{q})-Q_{p}^{-n}(q)\right]p_{1}Ia_{-2n+1}.

Formula (
10.30
) follows by putting together (
10.31
), (
10.35
), (
10.36
) and (
10.37
).
∎

11.
Applications to operator theory

Our work inserts in the field of the generalizations of the concept of holomorphicity in dimensions greater than one. Indeed, the set of holomorphic functions
f:\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
of one complex variable, denoted by
\mathcal{O}(\Omega)
, and the associated functional calculus admit extensions that are discussed below.

(I) The system of Cauchy-Riemann equations for functions
f:\Pi\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}\to\mathbb{C}
gives the theory of holomorphic functions in several complex variables.

The exploration of spectral theory based on the theory of several complex variables and their Cauchy formula gives rise to the so-called holomorphic functional calculus for
n
-tuples of operators and it is based on Taylor’s joint spectrum and its further developments.
This calculus was initiated in
[
86
,
87
,
88
]
, but see also
[
18
,
19
]
.

(II) The holomorphicity of vector fields is linked with quaternionic-valued functions and, more broadly, with Clifford algebra-valued functions. The Fueter-Sce-Qian theorem yields two distinct extensions namely two classes of hyperholomorphic functions as discussed in preceding sections.

Both these classes of functions possess a Cauchy formula applicable to defining functions of quaternionic operators or of
n
-tuples of (non-commuting) operators. More precisely:

(II-A) The Cauchy formula of slice hyperholomorphic functions leads to the
S
-functional calculus for quaternionic linear operators or for
n
-tuples of non-commuting operators, and more generally for Clifford operators. This calculus is grounded in the concept of the
S
-spectrum, on which the spectral theorem for quaternionic operators and for Clifford operators are based.

(II-B) The Cauchy formula of monogenic functions gives rise to the monogenic functional calculus, based on the monogenic spectrum. This calculus has also links with the Weyl functional calculus.

The functions systematically explored in this paper, along with their corresponding functional calculi, can collectively be referred to as quaternionic fine structures within the framework of the spectral theory based on the
S
-spectrum and belong to the developments in (II-A).
The integral representations of the functions of the fine structures are employed to introduce new functional calculi designed for quaternionic operators, encompassing both bounded and unbounded operators. It is worthwhile to note that, recently, these calculi have been extended to sectorial type operators.

As we have discussed in this work, the four distinct classes of functions in the quaternionic fine structures and their functional calculi are: slice hyperholomorphic functions (resulting in the
S
-functional calculus), axially harmonic functions (leading to the
Q
-functional calculus), axially polyanalytic functions of order
2
(resulting in the
P_{2}
-functional calculus), and axially Fueter regular functions (leading to the
F
-functional calculus). These calculi are based on the Cauchy formula for slice hyperholomorphic functions given by

f(q)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial(U\cap\mathbb{C}_{I})}S_{L}^{-1}(p,q)dp_{I}f(p),

where the (left) Cauchy kernel is considered in the second form, namely

S_{L}^{-1}(p,q):=(p-\overline{q})(p^{2}-2pq_{0}+|q|^{2})^{-1}.

Applying the operators of the quaternionic fine structure of Dirac type,
that is
D
,
\bar{D}
and
\Delta
to the Cauchy kernel
S_{L}^{-1}(p,q)
we obtain

(11.1)

\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-1}(q)=-\frac{1}{2}DS_{L}^{-1}(p,q),\qquad P_{2}^{L}(p,q)=\bar{D}S_{L}^{-1}(p,q),\qquad F_{L}(p,q)=\Delta S_{L}^{-1}(p,q),

and these new kernels give an integral representation of the functions of the quaternionic fine structure (see all the details in the
Subsection
2.5
on the integral representations of the functions of the fine structure of Dirac type).
We now recall the definition of
F
-spectrum associated with the spectral theory on the
S
-spectrum in its commutative version, that is associated with the Cauchy kernels in the second form.

Definition 11.1

(
F
-spectrum)
.

Let
T=T_{0}+T_{1}e_{1}+T_{2}e_{2}+T_{3}e_{3}
be a bounded quaternionic operator with commuting components
T_{\ell}
, for
\ell=0,...,3
and let
\overline{T}:=T_{0}-T_{1}e_{1}-T_{2}e_{2}-T_{3}e_{3}
be its conjugate and set
|T|^{2}=T\overline{T}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{3}T^{2}_{\ell}
. According to the invertibility of the operator

\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(T):=p^{2}-2pT_{0}+|T|^{2}

we define the
F
-resolvent set

(11.2)

\rho_{F}(T):=\Set{p\in\mathbb{H}}{\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}(T)\text{ is bijective}},

and the
F
-spectrum as the complement

(11.3)

\sigma_{F}(T):=\mathbb{H}\setminus\rho_{F}(T).

We observe that when dealing with bounded operators
T
, the
S
-spectrum

\sigma_{S}(T):=\Set{p\in\mathbb{H}}{Q_{p}(T)\text{ is not bijective}},\quad\text{with }Q_{p}(T):=T^{2}-2p_{0}T+|p|^{2},

coincides with the
F
-spectrum in (
11.3
). It is important to note that the
F
-spectrum can be viewed as a commutative counterpart of the
S
-spectrum, which is more general and it does not require that the components of the operator
T
commute among themselves.

The above definitions are the starting point to define:

- the

S
-functional calculus

f(T)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial(U\cap\mathbb{C}_{I})}S_{L}^{-1}(p,T)dp_{I}f(p),

- the
Q
-functional calculus, often called the harmonic functional calculus in the quaternionic setting

Df(T)=-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{\partial(U\cap\mathbb{C}_{I})}\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-1}(T)dp_{I}f(p),

- the
P_{2}
-functional calculus

\bar{D}f(T)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial(U\cap\mathbb{C}_{I})}P_{2}^{L}(p,T)dp_{I}f(p),

- the
F
-functional calculus

\Delta f(T)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{\partial(U\cap\mathbb{C}_{I})}F_{L}(p,T)dp_{I}f(p),

where
T
is a bounded quaternionic operator with commuting components.
The open set
U
contains the
S
-spectrum of
T
and all the above functional calculi
depend neither on
U
nor on the imaginary unit
I\in\mathbb{S}
, and in the case of the

Q
-functional calculus,
P_{2}
-functional calculus and the
F
-functional calculus, they do not depend on the kernels of the operators
D
,
\bar{D}
and
\Delta
, respectively.
The resolvent operators in the fine structure, i.e.,

S_{L}^{-1}(p,T)
,
\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-1}(T)
,
P_{2}^{L}(p,T)
and
F_{L}(p,T)
are obtained from the Cauchy kernel

S_{L}^{-1}(p,q)
and the kernels
\mathcal{Q}_{c,p}^{-1}(q)
,
P_{2}^{L}(p,q)
and
F_{L}(p,q)
of the functions of the fine structure given in (
11.1
) by replacing
q
with the operator
T
, respectively.

It is worthwhile to note that the
F
-functional calculus is a monogenic functional calculus in the spirit of the Cauchy formula of monogenic functions, but it is based on slice hyperholomorphic functions, via an integral transform, and on the
S
-spectrum (used instead of the monogenic spectrum to define a calculus via the monogenic Cauchy formula).

We remark that the fine structure on the
S
-spectrum is more suitable for operators with commuting components, whereas the
S
-functional calculus is naturally defined also for operators with noncommuting components.
The spectral theory on the
S
-spectrum
and all its variations arising from the fine structures on the
S
-spectrum
constitute natural spectral theories for vector operators.
An extension of the fine structures to Clifford algebras has been developed in
[
31
]
and
[
27
]
.

We finally highlight that the extension of the holomorphic functional calculus to sectorial operators leads to the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus, initially introduced in the complex context in the paper
[
74
]
and further discussed in the books
[
68
,
69
,
70
]
. This calculus has proven valuable in addressing boundary value problems, as evidenced by its applications highlighted in
[
11
,
12
,
13
]
. The boundedness of the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus relies on appropriate quadratic estimates while unbounded operators for a specific class of functions are discussed in
[
26
]
. The
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus for the quaternionic fine structures is treated in
[
38
,
53
]
.
It is worth noting that the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus is defined in the monogenic case as well, as detailed in
[
72
]
. This approach, pioneered by A. McIntosh and collaborators, is further explored in the books
[
71
,
82
]
.
